Enhanced dissipation and nonlinear asymptotic stability of the Taylor-Couette flow for the 2D Navier-Stokes equations
Xinliang AN, Taoran HE, Te LI

Abstract
. In this paper, we study the nonlinear stability of a steady circular flow created between two rotating concentric cylinders. The dynamics of the viscous fluid are described by 2D Navier-Stokes equations. We adopt scaling variables. For the rescaled equations, we prove that the steady flow (Taylor-Couette flow) is asymptotically stable up to a large perturbation of initial data. Back to the original 2D Navier-Stokes equations, this implies an improved transition threshold for the Taylor-Couette flow. The improvement is due to enhanced dissipation and new observations and constructions of weighted L 2 L^{2} norms, which capture a hidden structure between the viscosity constant ν \nu and (different) rotating speeds and locations of two coaxial cylinders. In particular, we allow the location of the outer cylinder to tend to infinity, which renders the initial fluid kinetic energy not uniformly bounded. Due to enhanced-dissipation effect, we also establish a sharp resolvent estimate, desired space-time bounds and optimal decaying estimates, which lead to the proof of nonlinear asymptotic stability of 2D Taylor-Couette flow.



1.
Introduction

In this paper, we consider the two-dimensional (2D) incompressible Navier-Stokes equations:

(1.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v-\nu\Delta v+v\cdot\nabla v+\nabla p=0,\quad\text{div }v=0,\\
&v(0,x)=v_{0}(x)\end{aligned}\right.

with
x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{2}
being the space variables and
t\geq 0
being the time variable. Here the constant
\nu>0
is called the kinematic viscosity. The unknowns to (
1.1
) are the velocity field
v(t,x)=(v^{1}(t,x),v^{2}(t,x))
and the pressure
p(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}
.

If taking the 2D curl to the above Navier-Stokes equations, then (
1.1
) is transferred to

(1.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\omega-\nu\Delta\omega+v\cdot\nabla\omega=0.

Here
\omega=\text{curl }v=\partial_{1}v^{2}-\partial_{2}v^{1}
is the vorticity field. For 2D case, it is a scalar. And (
1.2
) is called the vorticity equation formulation. In this paper, we will use (
1.2
) to understand the asymptotics of solutions to (
1.1
).

Given
\omega
being a solution to (
1.2
), the velocity field
v:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
can be derived via solving the below elliptic system

\displaystyle\text{div }v=0,\quad\text{curl }v=\omega.

This leads to the 2D Biot-Savart law

(1.3)

\displaystyle v(t,x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{(x-y)^{\perp}}{|x-y|^{2}}\omega(t,y)dy.

For notational simplicity, we write
v(t,\cdot)=K_{BS}\ast\omega(t,\cdot)
, where

K_{BS}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{x^{\perp}}{|x|^{2}}
is the Biot-Savart kernel.

1.1.
Taylor-Couette flow

In this article, we focus on studying the nonlinear stability and long-time dynamics around the
circular\ flows
with the vorticity
\omega(x)=\omega(|x|)
being radial. In below, we denote
\tilde{r}
to be
|x|
.

An important physical circular-flow solution to (
1.2
) is called the
Taylor-Couette flow
. It describes a steady (circular) flow of viscous fluid bounded between two rotating infinitely long coaxial cylinders and has wide applications ranging from desalination to viscometric analysis. Now we derive its expression.

Denote
\omega(x)=\omega(\tilde{r})
and
\phi(x)=\phi(\tilde{r})
to be the radial vorticity and the stream function, respectively. Then for 2D case we have

(1.4)

\displaystyle\omega(\tilde{r})=\Delta\phi=\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{r})+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{r}),

\displaystyle v(x,y)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\partial_{2}\phi\\
\partial_{1}\phi\\
\end{array}\right)=\partial_{\tilde{r}}\phi e_{\tilde{\theta}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-x_{2}\\
x_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)\frac{\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{r})}{\tilde{r}}.

One can easily check
\nabla\cdot v=0
and via
\partial_{1}=\cos\tilde{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\sin\tilde{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{\theta}}
,
\partial_{2}=\sin\tilde{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{r}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\cos\tilde{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{\theta}}
, one also has

\displaystyle v\cdot\nabla\omega=-\sin\tilde{\theta}\phi^{\prime}\partial_{1}\omega+\cos\tilde{\theta}\phi^{\prime}\partial_{2}\omega=0.

Hence, a radial vorticity
\omega(\tilde{r})
in (
1.4
) is a stationary solution to the below 2D incompressible Euler equation

(1.9)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\omega+v\cdot\nabla\omega=0.

In particular, if we choose
\omega=\textrm{const}
, then we have
\Delta\omega=0
and
\omega
is also a solution to the 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes equation (
1.2
). For this case, by (
1.4
) the stream function
\phi(\tilde{r})
reads

(1.10)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{r})+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{r})=2A_{1}.

where
A_{1},A_{2}
are constants. This implies
(\tilde{r}\phi^{\prime})^{\prime}=2A_{1}\tilde{r}
and
\frac{\phi^{\prime}}{\tilde{r}}=A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{\tilde{r}^{2}}
.

Via (
1.10
) we now derive a solution
v(x,y)
to (
1.1
). And with polar coordinate, we rename
v(x,y)
and
\omega(r)
to be
U(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})
and
\Omega(\tilde{r})
, respectively. Now we have

\displaystyle U(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}U^{1}\\
U^{2}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\sin\tilde{\theta}\\
\cos\tilde{\theta}\\
\end{array}\right)(A_{1}\tilde{r}+\frac{A_{2}}{\tilde{r}})=v_{\tilde{\theta}}e_{\tilde{\theta}},\quad\Omega(\tilde{r})=2A_{1},

where
A_{1},A_{2}
are constants,
e_{\tilde{\theta}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\sin\tilde{\theta}\\
\cos\tilde{\theta}\\
\end{array}\right)
, and
v_{\tilde{\theta}}=A_{1}\tilde{r}+\frac{A_{2}}{\tilde{r}}
is the azimuthal velocity component. Note that
U(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})
in (
1.1
) is a stationary solution to (
1.1
) and it is called the
Taylor-Couette flow
.

1.2.
Connection and comparison to the Lamb-Oseen vortex

It is well known that the 2D Navier-Stokes equations (
1.2
) have a family of self-similar solutions called the Lamb-Oseen vortices, which are of the following form

(1.15)

\displaystyle\omega(t,x)=\frac{\alpha}{\nu t}G\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}}\Big),\quad v(t,x)=\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{\nu t}}v^{G}\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}}\Big).

Here the vorticity profile and the velocity profile are given by

(1.16)

\displaystyle G(\xi)=\frac{1}{4\pi}e^{-|\xi|^{2}/4},\quad v^{G}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\xi^{\perp}}{|\xi|^{2}}\Big(1-e^{-|\xi|^{2}/4}\Big).

In particular we have
v^{G}=K_{BS}\ast G
with
K_{BS}
being the Biot-Savart kernel. And we further denote space

(1.17)

\displaystyle Y:=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},G^{-1}d\xi).

An important property of flow obeying (
1.2
) is that it preserves the mass in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, i.e.,

(1.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega(t,x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega(0,x)dx=\alpha\quad\textrm{for any}\ t>0.

The parameter
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
is called the circulation Reynolds number. People studying fluid mechanics are especially interested in rapidly rotating vortices, where the circulation
|\alpha|
is much larger compared with the kinematic viscosity
\nu
. This is the regime most
relevant with applications to 2D turbulent flows. Recently, there are exciting progress on the linear and nonlinear stability of the Lamb-Oseen vortex. The results in this article are parallel to these developments. And here we review some important results about the Lamb-Oseen vortex.

In 2005, to study the long-time dynamics of the 2D Navier-Stokes equations, Gallay and Wayne
[
23
]
introduced the so called
scaling variables
or
similarity variables
:

(1.19)

\displaystyle\xi=\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\quad\tau=\log t.

And they work under the ansatz

(1.20)

\displaystyle\omega(x,t)=\frac{1}{t}w(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\log t),\quad v(x,t)=\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{t}}u(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\log t).

The rescaled vorticity
w(\xi,\tau)
now satisfies the evolution equation

(1.21)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w-(\Delta_{\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}+1)w+u\cdot\nabla_{\xi}w=0.

When the initial vorticity is integrable, i.e., when
\alpha
defined in (
1.18
) is finite, Gallay and Wayne
[
22
,
23
]
proved that the long-time dynamical behaviours of the 2D Navier-Stokes equations can be described and approximated by the Lamb-Oseen vortex.
Let
G
be defined as in (
1.16
). They proved

Proposition 1.1

(Gallay-Wayne
[
23
]
)
.

For any
w_{0}\in Y
, the rescaled vorticity equation (
1.21
) admits a unique global mild solution
w\in C^{0}([0,\infty),Y)
with
w(0)=w_{0}
. This solution satisfies
\|w(\tau)-\alpha G\|_{Y}\to 0
as
\tau\to+\infty
, where
\alpha=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w_{0}(\xi)d\xi
.

Later in 2008, Gallay and Rodrigues
[
18
,
20
]
showed that for any finite value of circulation parameter
\alpha
, the equilibrium
\alpha G
is asymptotically stable in
Y
.

Proposition 1.2

(Gallay-Rodrigues
[
18
,
20
]
)
.

There exists
\epsilon>0
such that, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
being finite and for all
w_{0}\in\alpha G+Y_{0}
with
Y_{0}=\{w\in Y|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w(\xi)d\xi=0\}
, if requiring
\|w_{0}-\alpha G\|_{Y}\leq\epsilon
, then the unique solution to (
1.21
) with initial data
w_{0}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|w(\cdot,\tau)-\alpha G\|_{Y}\leq\min(1,2e^{-\frac{\tau}{2}})\|w_{0}-\alpha G\|_{Y}\quad\textrm{for any}\ \tau\geq 0.

A feature associated with (
1.2
) that Proposition
1.2
did not address is the enhanced dissipation effect when the circulation Reynolds numbers
|\alpha|
is large. Gallay proposed a conjecture on the optimal resolvent estimate for the Oseen vortex. And this conjecture was solved by Li, Wei and Zhang in
[
29
]
. Let
G
and
v^{G}
be defined as in (
1.16
). Set
L
to be
\Delta_{\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}+1

and operator
\Lambda
to satisfy

\displaystyle\Lambda w=v^{G}\cdot\nabla w+u\cdot\nabla G\quad\textrm{with}\ u=K_{BS}\ast w.

Note that
\Lambda
is a nonlocal linear operator. Then the following statement holds

Proposition 1.3

(Li-Wei-Zhang
[
29
]
)
.

Denote
L_{\bot}
and
\Lambda_{\bot}
to be
L
and
\Lambda
’s restriction to the orthogonal complement of ker
\Lambda
in Y. Define the below pseudospectral bound

\displaystyle\Psi(\alpha):=(\sup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}}\|(L_{\bot}-\alpha\Lambda_{\bot}-i\lambda)^{-1}\|_{Y\to Y})^{-1}.

Then as
|\alpha|\to+\infty
, there exists
C>0
independent of
\alpha
satisfying

\displaystyle C^{-1}|\alpha|^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq\Psi(\alpha)\leq C|\alpha|^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Based on the linear resolvent estimate in
[
29
]
, Gallay
[
19
]
proved an improved stability result which allows the size of perturbation (size of the basin of attraction) of Oseen’s vortex to be large when
|\alpha|\gg 1
. Also, the non-zero frequency of vorticity obeys a faster decaying rate. Rewrite
w(\xi,\tau)
as
w(\theta,r,\tau)
with polar coordinate and let space
Y_{1}
be
\{w\in Y|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\xi_{i}w(\xi)d\xi=0,i=1,2\}
. It holds

Proposition 1.4

(Gallay
[
19
]
)
.

There exist positive constants
C_{1},C_{2}
,
\kappa
such that, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and all initial data
w_{0}\in\alpha G+Y_{1}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|w_{0}-\alpha G\|_{Y}\leq\frac{C_{1}(1+|\alpha|)^{\frac{1}{6}}}{\log(2+|\alpha|)},

we have that the unique solution to (
1.21
) in
Y
(guaranteed by Proposition
1.1
) obeys, for any
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|w(\cdot,\tau)-\alpha G\|_{Y}\leq C_{2}e^{-\tau}\|w_{0}-\alpha G\|_{Y},

\displaystyle\|(1-P_{r})w(\cdot,\tau)-\alpha G\|_{Y}\leq C_{2}\|w_{0}-\alpha G\|_{Y}\exp(-\frac{\kappa(1+|\alpha|)^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}{\log(2+|\alpha|)}),

where

\displaystyle P_{r}w(\theta,r,\tau)=\int_{0}^{2\pi}w(\theta,r,\tau)d\theta.

1.3.
Main results

In this paper, we study fluid dynamics around the Taylor-Couette flow. We are in particular interested in the regime with a high Reynolds number (
\textrm{Re}=\frac{1}{\nu}
).

The Taylor-Couette flow is the steady flow created between two rotating concentric cylinders. In this paper, we consider the 2D Taylor-Couette flow, and aim to prove its nonlinear asymptotic stability under the 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations (
1.2
). For the Taylor-Couette flow, if the inner cylinder with radius
r_{1}
is rotating at constant angular velocity
\omega_{1}
and the outer cylinder with radius
r_{2}
is rotating at constant angular velocity
\omega_{2}
, then the azimuthal velocity component is given by

(1.22)

\displaystyle v_{\theta}=A_{1}r+\frac{A_{2}}{r}=r\omega_{phy},\quad A_{1}=\omega_{1}\frac{\mu-\eta^{2}}{1-\eta^{2}},\quad A_{2}=\omega_{1}r_{1}^{2}\frac{1-\mu}{1-\eta^{2}},

where

\displaystyle\mu=\frac{\omega_{2}}{\omega_{1}},\quad\eta=\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}},\quad\omega_{phy}:=A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{r^{2}}.

One can verify

\displaystyle\omega_{1}=\omega_{phy}(r_{1}),\quad\omega_{2}=\omega_{phy}(r_{2}).

Then we can directly compute fluid vorticity from the velocity field as below

\displaystyle\Omega(r)=

\displaystyle\partial_{1}U^{2}-\partial_{2}U^{1}=\partial_{1}(x_{1}\omega_{phy})-\partial_{2}(-x_{2}\omega_{phy})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\omega_{phy}+x_{1}\partial_{1}\omega_{phy}+x_{2}\partial_{2}\omega_{phy}=2\omega_{phy}+r\omega_{phy}^{\prime}=2A_{1}.

Although
\omega_{phy}
is singular at
r=0
, the above equality indicates that
\Omega(r)
is regular at
r=0
.

Before presenting our main theorems, let’s review some related results on the mathematical study of the Taylor-Couette flow or more general circular flows:

•

In
[
47
]
, for 2D incompressible Euler equations, Zillinger established linear inviscid damping with decay rates around the Taylor-Couette flow in an annular region. Note that this restriction on the annual region also avoid the issues of fluid dynamics at the origin and at infinity.

•

In
[
46
]
, for 2D Euler equations, Zelati and Zillinger established linear inviscid damping and proved linear stability for a class of mildly degenerate flows. This class includes
U(r)\sim\frac{1}{r}+r
, which is different from shear flows with strict monotonicity
[
41
]
.

•

Recently, Gallay and
\check{S}
ver
\acute{a}
k
[
21
]
employed variational principles (related to Arnold’s stability criteria) to study orbital stability of steady circular solutions to both Euler’s equations and the Navier-Stokes equation at low viscosity. They proved the stability for the algebraic vortex
\omega(x)=(1+|x|^{2})^{-\kappa}
with
\kappa>1
and for the Lamb-Oseen vortex
\omega(x)=e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4}}
. Note that the vorticity of the Taylor-Couette flow does not decay when
|x|\to+\infty
. And it has not been addressed in
[
21
]
.

We proceed to state our main results. In this paper, we work with scaling variables:

\displaystyle\xi=\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\quad\tau=\log t.

In
[
23
]
, Gallay and Wayne rewrite solutions to (
1.2
) under the ansatz

\displaystyle\omega(x,t)=\frac{1}{t}w(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\log t),\quad v(x,t)=\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{t}}u(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu t}},\log t).

Then the rescaled vorticity
w(\xi,\tau)
satisfies the following evolution equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w-(\Delta_{\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}+1)w+u\cdot\nabla_{\xi}w=0.

The rescaled velocity
u
can be expressed via the Biot-Savart law, namely
u(\cdot,\tau)=K_{BS}\ast w(\cdot,\tau)
. If we write
\xi
in polar coordinates

\displaystyle\xi_{1}=r\cos\theta,\quad\xi_{2}=r\sin\theta\quad\textrm{with}\ r\in[0,\infty)\quad\textrm{and}\ \theta\in\mathbb{T}.

Then we can take Fourier transform with respect to
\theta
. Denoting
w_{k}(\tau,r)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}w(\tau,r,\theta)e^{-ik\theta}d\theta,
we can write

\displaystyle w(\tau,\xi)=w(\tau,r,\theta)=\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\omega_{k}(\tau,r)e^{ik\theta}.

Recall that the fluid velocity
U
and vorticity
\Omega
of the Taylor-Couette flow (
1.1
) are given by

\displaystyle U(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}U^{1}\\
U^{2}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\sin\tilde{\theta}\\
\cos\tilde{\theta}\\
\end{array}\right)(A_{1}\tilde{r}+\frac{A_{2}}{\tilde{r}})=v_{\tilde{\theta}}e_{\tilde{\theta}},\quad\Omega(r)=2A_{1}.

Here
A_{1},A_{2}
are constants,
e_{\tilde{\theta}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\sin\tilde{\theta}\\
\cos\tilde{\theta}\\
\end{array}\right)
and
v_{\tilde{\theta}}
is the azimuthal velocity component. Via the self-similar transformation, the steady circular solutions (
1.1
) to the original (
1.9
) equation are concerted to

\displaystyle V=\sqrt{\frac{t}{\nu}}U=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\xi_{2}\\
\xi_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)(A_{1}e^{\tau}+\frac{A_{2}}{\nu|\xi|^{2}}),\quad W=t\Omega=2A_{1}e^{\tau}.

For 2D Navier-Stokes equations, we work with scaling variables and its equivalent form (
1.21
). Thus the original problem is transformed into analyzing the stability of (
1.3
) with
equation (
1.21
). For initial data, via Fourier series we have
w(0,r,\theta)=w_{0}(0,r)+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}w_{k}(0,r)e^{ik\theta}
. For simplicity, we also employ
w(0),w_{0}(0),w_{k}(0)
to replace
w(0,r,\theta),w_{0}(0,r),w_{k}(0,r)
, respectively.

For future use, we define

(1.25)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}:=\int_{0}^{\infty}re^{\frac{r^{2}}{4}}|w(r)|^{2}dr,\quad\quad\|w\|_{X}:=(\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|w|^{2}}{r^{2}}dr)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

And we introduce an energy norm
\mathcal{E}(\tau)
as below

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\tau)=|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{\omega_{0}(\tau)-2A_{1}e^{\tau}}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}(\|\omega_{k}(\tau)\|_{\mathcal{M}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{6}}|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{\omega_{k}(\tau)}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}}).

For initial data, via Fourier series we have
w(0,r,\theta)=w_{0}(0,r)+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}w_{k}(0,r)e^{ik\theta}
. For simplicity, we also adapt
w(0),w_{0}(0),w_{k}(0)
to denote
w(0,r,\theta),w_{0}(0,r),w_{k}(0,r)
, respectively. In our paper, we are interested in the regime when
\nu\leq|A_{2}|
. In particular, we allow
0<\nu\ll 1
. Our main result of this paper can be stated as follows:

Theorem 1.5

(Main Theorem)
.

For any
|A_{2}|\geq\nu
, there exist constants
c_{0},c,C>0
independent of
A_{1},A_{2},\nu
such that if the initial data
w(0)=w_{0}(0)+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}w_{k}(0)e^{ik\theta}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(0)=|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{\omega_{0}(0)-2A_{1}}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}(\|\omega_{k}(0)\|_{\mathcal{M}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{6}}|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{\omega_{k}(0)}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}})\leq c_{0}|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}},

then the solution
w
of the full nonlinear vorticity formulation (
1.21
) is global in time and the following stability estimate holds

\displaystyle|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{e^{-\tau}\omega_{0}(\tau)-2A_{1}}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}}\leq Ce^{-\tau}\mathcal{E}(0),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\}}(\|\omega_{k}(\tau)\|_{\mathcal{M}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{6}}|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{\omega_{k}(\tau)}{r}\|_{\mathcal{M}})\leq Ce^{-c|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\mathcal{E}(0).

Hence, it holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\tau)\leq C\mathcal{E}(0).

In our work, we keep tracking the relation between the enhanced dissipation effect and the coefficients
\nu
and
A_{1},A_{2}
. We are especially interested in the case with rapidly moving velocity, where the parameter
|A_{2}|
is much larger than the kinematic viscosity
\nu
.

Remark 1.6
.

Compared with Proposition
1.4
stated above by
[
19
]
, here we allow larger perturbation. In
[
19
]
for the Oseen’s vortex, the size of the basin for attraction is of
|\alpha|^{\frac{1}{6}}
(where
\alpha\sim\nu^{-1})
, while here for Taylor-Couette flow,
|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|
is analogous to
|\alpha|
, and the size of perturbation is permitted to be of size
|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. Moreover, to control the nonlinear terms in a sharper way, we construct and employ a designed weighted energy space
X
with
\|w\|_{X}=(\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|w|^{2}}{r^{2}}dr)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. This can be fulfilled due to a newly found connection about order of
|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|
between two space-time bounds. Tentative space-time estimates leads us to consider the below two sums

(1.26)

\displaystyle\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\boxed{\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}}+\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}(\frac{|k|}{r}+r)w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}},

(1.27)

\displaystyle\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}X}+|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}+\boxed{\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}rw_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}}.

And we expect (
1.26
) and
\eqref{2-e}\cdot|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}
are of the same order. Note that in (
1.27
) the coefficient of
\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}rw_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}=\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
is
1
, and in (
1.26
) the coefficient in front of
\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
is
|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}
. This observation motivates us to couple
L^{2}
and
X
space together to construct combined energy
E_{k}(|k|\geq 1)
of order
\eqref{1-e}+|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\eqref{2-e}
as below

(1.28)

\displaystyle E_{k}=

\displaystyle\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\Big(\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w\|_{L^{\infty}X}

\displaystyle+|k|\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}}\Big)+|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c|\frac{kA_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w\|_{L^{2}X}.

It turns out that
E_{k}
is the correct energy, which enables us to prove the desired result with improvements. If we only adapt the natural
L^{2}
energy (
1.26
) as in
[
19
]
, we can prove a stability result allowing perturbation of initial data is less then
(\frac{A_{2}}{\nu})^{\frac{1}{6}}
, while with
X
space and
E_{k}
energy, we allow the perturbation to be less than
|\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. By establishing sharp resolvent estimates and employing Gearhart-Pr
\ddot{u}
ss type theorem in
[
39
]
, we also avoid the loss of
\log|\alpha|
in Proposition
1.4
.

Remark 1.7
.

To prove the nonlinear result Theorem
1.5
, we also obtain a sharp pseudospectral bound
(\frac{A_{2}}{\nu})^{\frac{1}{3}}
for the linearized operator around the Taylor-Couette flow in Section
2
. This sharp bound is consistent with the estimates for the Lamb-Oseen vortices operator in
[
29
]
by Li-Wei-Zhang. The obtained pseudospectral bound then implies a sharp enhanced dissipation decaying rate
e^{-(\frac{A_{2}}{\nu})^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}
for the vorticity equation. Not only we give resolvent estimates from
L^{2}
space to
L^{2}
space, we also establish the counterpart from
L^{2}
space to
H^{-1}
space and extend the optimal resolvent estimate to the weighted
L^{2}
space. These estimates together infer desired space-time bounds for nonlinear terms in Section
2
.

Translated back to the original equations (
1.2
), initial data at
\tau=0
is corresponding to data at
t=1
. The above main theorem implies the below improved transition threshold result:

Theorem 1.8
.

(Transition Threshold) For any
|A_{2}|\geq\nu
, there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
independent of
A_{1},A_{2},\nu
such that if the initial data at
t=1
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\omega(1)-2A_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{1}|A_{2}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\nu^{\frac{2}{3}},

then the solution
\omega(t)
to the nonlinear vorticity formulation (
1.2
) is global in time and obeys the following stability estimate

\displaystyle\|\omega(t)-2A_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{2}|A_{2}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}.

Remark 1.9
.

In this theorem,
|A_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}
of order
\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}
is the transition threshold. Recall on page 6 of
[
19
]
Gallay proved (in
L^{1}
norm) a transition threshold of order
\nu^{\frac{5}{6}}
for the flow near the Lamb-Oseen vortices. By scaling consideration, we expect consistent transition thresholds for the Lamb-Oseen vortices and for the Taylor-Couette flow. Our improvement comes from the desired estimates in weighted norms obtained in Theorem
1.5
. In particular, we overcome the difficulty for the cases when
r_{1}\to 0
and
r_{2}\to+\infty
.

1.4.
Derivation of equations

For notational simplicity, in below we write
A_{1}
and
\frac{A_{2}}{\nu}
as
A
and
B
respectively. And we also use polar coordinates

\displaystyle\xi_{1}=r\cos\theta,\quad\xi_{2}=r\sin\theta\quad\textrm{with}\ r\in[0,\infty)\quad\textrm{and}\ \theta\in\mathbb{T}.

Define
\tilde{w}=w-W
,
\tilde{u}=u-V
with
W
and
V
given in (
1.3
). Via (
1.21
) and
\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}W=0
, we have

(1.29)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{w}-(\Delta_{\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}+1)\tilde{w}+(\tilde{u}+V)\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\tilde{w}=0.

Since
\partial_{\xi_{1}}=\cos\theta\partial_{r}-\frac{1}{r}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}
and
\partial_{\xi_{2}}=\sin\theta\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r}\cos\theta\partial_{\theta}
, we deduce

\displaystyle-\xi_{2}\partial_{\xi_{1}}+\xi_{1}\partial_{\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\theta},\quad\xi_{1}\partial_{\xi_{1}}+\xi_{2}\partial_{\xi_{2}}=r\partial_{r},\quad\Delta_{\xi}=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2}.

Thus, equation (
1.29
) becomes

(1.30)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{w}-[(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}+1]\tilde{w}+(Ae^{\tau}+\frac{B}{r^{2}})\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\tilde{w}=0.

Recall we have
\tilde{u}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\partial_{\xi_{2}}\varphi\\
\partial_{\xi_{1}}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)
with
\varphi
being the stream function satisfying

\displaystyle\Delta_{\xi}\varphi=(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2})\varphi=\tilde{w}.

Then it follows

\displaystyle\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\tilde{w}=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\xi_{2}}\varphi\partial_{\xi_{1}}\tilde{w}+\partial_{\xi_{1}}\varphi\partial_{\xi_{2}}\tilde{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\sin\theta\partial_{r}\varphi+\frac{1}{r}\cos\theta\partial_{\theta}\varphi)(\cos\theta\partial_{r}\tilde{w}-\frac{1}{r}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w})

\displaystyle+(\cos\theta\partial_{r}\varphi-\frac{1}{r}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\varphi)(\sin\theta\partial_{r}\tilde{w}+\frac{1}{r}\cos\theta\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}(\partial_{r}\varphi\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w}-\partial_{\theta}\varphi\partial_{r}\tilde{w}).

Together with

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\varphi\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w}-\partial_{\theta}\varphi\partial_{r}\tilde{w}=\partial_{r}(\varphi\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w})-\partial_{\theta}(\varphi\partial_{r}\tilde{w}),

we derive the below (nonlinear) perturbation equation (
1.30
) for
\tilde{w}

(1.31)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\tilde{w}

\displaystyle-[(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}+1]\tilde{w}+(Ae^{\tau}+\frac{B}{r^{2}})\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}[\partial_{r}(\varphi\partial_{\theta}\tilde{w})-\partial_{\theta}(\varphi\partial_{r}\tilde{w})]=0.

Take the Fourier transform in
\theta
direction. Denote the Fourier coefficient of
\tilde{w}
and
\varphi
to be
\hat{w}_{k}
and
\hat{\varphi}_{k}
, respectively. Then equation (
1.31
) becomes

(1.32)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\hat{w}_{k}

\displaystyle-[(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}})+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}+1]\hat{w}_{k}+(Ae^{\tau}+\frac{B}{r^{2}})ik\hat{w}_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\hat{w}_{l}\hat{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{\varphi}_{k-l}\partial_{r}\hat{w}_{l}]=0

with
\hat{\varphi}_{k}
satisfying
(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}})\hat{\varphi}_{k}=\hat{w}_{k}
.

Denote
\breve{w}_{k}:=e^{ikAe^{\tau}}\hat{w}_{k}
and
\breve{\varphi}_{k}:=e^{ikAe^{\tau}}\hat{\varphi}_{k}
. We obtain an equivalent form for (
1.32
)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\breve{w}_{k}

\displaystyle-[(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}})+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}+1]\breve{w}_{k}+\frac{ikB}{r^{2}}\breve{w}_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}\partial_{r}\breve{w}_{l}]=0

with
\breve{\varphi}_{k}
satisfying
(\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}})\breve{\varphi}_{k}=\breve{w}_{k}
.

Let us further define

w_{k}:=f^{-1}\breve{w}_{k}
with
f=\frac{e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{8}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}
.
This
f^{-1}
weight is designed to make the first derivative
\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}
vanish. With
w_{k}
we deduce

(1.33)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w_{k}

\displaystyle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w_{k}+\frac{ikB}{r^{2}}w_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}\partial_{r}\breve{w}_{l}]=0.

Next we give the explicit forms of nonlinear terms. We first write

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}\partial_{r}\breve{w}_{l}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\partial_{r}(\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}\breve{w}_{l})+ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{w}_{l}\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{w}_{l}\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}-\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})],

Observing and utilizing below basic equalites

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})=\partial_{r}(\frac{f^{-1}}{\tilde{r}}\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-\partial_{r}(\frac{f^{-1}}{\tilde{r}})\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l},\quad\partial_{r}f=-(\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{4}r)f,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(\frac{f^{-1}}{r})=-\frac{f^{\prime}}{f^{2}}\frac{1}{r}-\frac{f^{-1}}{r^{2}}=(\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{4}r)f^{-1}\frac{1}{r}-\frac{f^{-1}}{r^{2}}=(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}})f^{-1},

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}il\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})-ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}\partial_{r}\breve{w}_{l}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f^{-1}}{r}[ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\breve{w}_{l}\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}-\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)\partial_{r}(\breve{w}_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}w_{l}\frac{\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r}-\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)(\partial_{r}(\frac{w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r})-(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}})w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}w_{l}\frac{\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r}+\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}})w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}-\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)\partial_{r}(\frac{w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r})].

We denote

\displaystyle f_{1}:=ik\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}w_{l}\frac{\partial_{r}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r}+\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}})w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l},

\displaystyle f_{2}:=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}i(k-l)\frac{w_{l}\breve{\varphi}_{k-l}}{r}.

Then the nonlinear perturbation equation (
1.33
) can be written as

(1.34)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}w_{k}+L_{k}w_{k}+f_{1}-\partial_{r}f_{2}=0,\\
&w_{k}(0)=w_{k}|_{\tau=0},\quad w_{k}|_{\partial\Gamma}=0\end{aligned}\right.

with
\partial\Gamma
being the boundaries of the viscous fluid. Here

\displaystyle L_{k}=-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]+i\frac{kB}{r^{2}}.

1.5.
Strategy and structure of the paper

In Section
2
, we establish the resolvent estimate for the linearized equation

\displaystyle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w=F,\quad w|_{r=0}=w|_{r=\infty}=0

in both
L^{2}
space and weighted
L^{2}
space
X
. Here
X
is defined in (
1.25
).
The resolvent estimate is inspired by
[
29
]
. A key difference to
[
29
]
is that, for here, not only we give the resolvent estimate from
L^{2}
to
L^{2}
, we also establish the resolvent estimate from
L^{2}
to
H^{-1}
. And we further generalize and extend these resolvent estimates to constructed weighted spaces as well. Our estimates in weighted spaces enable us to sharpen previous results and get the desired nonlinear theorem. In later sections to derive a sharp decaying estimate
e^{-c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}
for the nonlinear problem, we also need to shift the linear operator to the left by
c_{2}|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. This means that we establish bounds for
\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{L^{2}}
,
\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}
,
\|\frac{F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}
and
\|\frac{F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w}{r}\|_{H^{-1}}
. Besides dodging a potential
\log|B|
loss, we also avoid proving the resolvent estimate between
\|w\|_{H^{-1}}
and
\|F\|_{H^{-1}}
.

In Section
3
, we derive space-time estimates for the linearized Navier-Stokes equations. In the aim of applying these estimates to the nonlinear problem, we first study the following inhomogeneous equation (
1.34
) for
w_{k}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w_{k}+L_{k}w_{k}+f_{1}-\partial_{r}f_{2}=0,\quad w_{k}(0)=w_{k}|_{\tau=0}.

We decompose
w_{k}
into two parts with
w_{k}=w_{k}^{l}+w_{k}^{n}
. Here
w_{k}^{l}
satisfies the below homogeneous linear equation (
3.2
) with initial data
w_{k}(0)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w_{k}^{l}+L_{k}w_{k}^{l}=0,\quad w_{k}^{l}(0)=w_{k}(0),

and
w_{k}^{n}
verifies the inhomogeneous linear equation (
3.7
) with zero initial data

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}w_{k}^{n}+L_{k}w_{k}^{n}+f_{1}-\partial_{r}f_{2}=0,\quad w_{k}^{n}(0)=0.

We obtain the sharp bound for
w_{k}^{l}
by using Gearhart-Pr
\ddot{u}
ss type theorem in
[
39
]
to avoid
\log|B|
, since the linearized operator
L_{k}
is accretive in both
L^{2}
space and weighted
L^{2}
space. Through Fourier transform and applying proofs in Proposition
2.7
and Proposition
2.10
, we derive a sharp semigroup bound for
w_{k}^{n}
as well. Putting together, our space-time estimates take the below forms:

\displaystyle M(L^{2}):=

\displaystyle\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}\boxed{\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}}

\displaystyle+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\partial_{r}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}(\frac{|k|}{r}+r)w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(|kB|^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}f_{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|w_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

and

\displaystyle M(X):=

\displaystyle\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}X}+|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}

\displaystyle+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\partial_{r}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}+|k|\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r}\|_{L^{2}X}+\boxed{\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}rw_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(|kB|^{-\frac{1}{6}}(\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}X}+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{f_{2}}{r}\|_{L^{2}X})+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}f_{2}\|_{L^{2}X}+\|w_{k}(0)\|_{X}\Big).

The terms singled out in
M(L^{2})
and
M(X)
will help us to design the correct “energy norm”
E_{k}
used in Section
4
.

In Section
4
, we control the nonlinear terms in a sharpen way and employ a designed weighted energy space. Recall the expression of
M(L^{2})
and
M(X)
. In
M(X)
, the coefficient of
\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}rw_{k}\|_{L^{2}X}=\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
is
1
, and in
M(L^{2})
the coefficient of
\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
is
|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}
. This suggests that a desired energy norm should be of order
M(L^{2})+|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}M(X)
. And it motivates us to couple
L^{2}
and
X
spaces together to construct the combined energy
E_{k}(k\in\mathbb{Z}/\{0\})
as below

\displaystyle E_{k}:=

\displaystyle\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}\Big(\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+|k|\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}}\Big)+|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}.

With these
E_{k}
, we prove our main conclusion-Theorem
4.1
.

Note that the last term
|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
in
E_{k}
plays a crucial role when estimating nonlinear terms. It is because in
E_{k}
we utilize
|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
rather than
|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}w_{k}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
to control nonlinear terms, that we allow larger
|B|^{\frac{1}{3}}
(rather than
|B|^{\frac{1}{6}}
) perturbation in initial data. Furthermore, our argument is acompanied by including a

|kB|^{\frac{1}{6}}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|e^{c|kB|^{\frac{1}{3}}\tau}\frac{w_{k}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}}
term in
E_{k}
, which helps to control weights in
k
.

1.6.
Other related results

Here we list some other related works about enhanced dissipation and nonlinear asymptotic stability in 2D.

For 2D Navier-Stokes, enhanced dissipation effect was proved by Beck-Wayne in
[
1
]
for a passive scalar advected by the Kolmogorov flow. The case of
u=(u(y),0)
with a finite number of critical points was treated by Bedrossian-Zelati in
[
7
]
. The case of the Kolmogorov flow was analyzed by Lin-Xu in
[
30
]
, Ibrahim-Maekawa-Masmoudi in
[
24
]
and Wei-Zhang-Zhao in
[
43
]
, and they obtained a time-scale of order
O(\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. In
[
45
]
, Zelati-Delgadino-Elgindi explored its connection to mixing effect. In
[
10
]
, for the linearized Navier-Stokes system around monotone shear flows with non-slip condition, Chen-Wei-Zhang further developed estimates allowing the couple of the enhanced dissipation and the inviscid-damping effects.

For the 2D Boussinesq equations, in
[
15
]
Deng-Wu-Zhang proved enhanced dissipation via studying a non-self-adjoint operator. We also want to refer to a result achieving to establish nonlinear inviscid damping for 2D Euler equations by Ionescu-Jia
[
25
]
. There they proved nonlinear asymptotic stability of monotonic shear flows in the channel.

1.7.
Acknowledgments

The authors would like to thank Professor Zhifei Zhang for valueable discussions and correspondences.

2.
Resolvent estimates in
L^{2}
space and in weighted
L^{2}
space

In order to establish decays of the linearized equation (
1.34
), the first step is to study the resolvent equation under the following boundary condition in both
L^{2}
space and weighted
L^{2}
space. More precisely, we consider

(2.1)

\displaystyle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w=F\quad\textrm{with}\ w|_{r=0,\infty}=0,

where
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. The domain is defined as

\displaystyle D_{k}=\{w\in H_{loc}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},dr)\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},dr):-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w+i\frac{\beta_{k}}{r^{2}}w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},dr)\}.

Note that for any
|k|\geq 1
, it holds

\displaystyle D_{k}

\displaystyle=\{w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},dr):\partial_{r}^{2}w,\frac{w}{r^{2}},r^{2}w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},dr)\}.

We start with deriving the coercive estimates for
\Re\langle F,w\rangle
and
\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle
, which are the real part of
\langle F,w\rangle
and
\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle
, respectively.

2.1.
Coercive estimates of the real part

Lemma 2.1
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
and
w\in D_{k}
, it holds

(2.2)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

Proof.

We prove this lemma by considering the following two scenarios:

•

When
|k|=1
, one can check

\displaystyle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w=-h^{-1}\partial_{r}[h^{2}\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)]+\frac{1}{2}w,

where
h=r^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{8}}
. Via integration by parts we obtain

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle=\Re\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w,w\rangle=\|h\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which implies

(2.3)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\geq\frac{1}{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

We also have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle=\|r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{1}{2}}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle\geq\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle.

Adding these two inequalities, we deduce

(2.4)

\displaystyle 2\Re\langle F,w\rangle\geq\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16})w,w\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

Applying integration by parts one also obtains

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle=

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle(\frac{1}{4r^{2}}+\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle.

Thus we get

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\langle(\frac{1}{4r^{2}}+\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle+\Re\langle F,w\rangle\lesssim\Re\langle F,w\rangle.

Combining with (
2.4
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

•

When
|k|\geq 2
, observing

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2}\geq\frac{2}{3}\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2}\gtrsim\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2},

and via employing integration by parts again, we conclude

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle=\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,w\rangle\gtrsim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

This completes the proof of Lemma
2.1
.
∎

With Lemma
2.1
, we then can establish

Lemma 2.2
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
and
w\in D_{k}
, it holds

(2.5)

\displaystyle|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}+|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{2}},

(2.6)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F\|_{H^{-1}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle F,w\rangle,

which implies

\displaystyle|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we deduce

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle F,w\rangle\lesssim|k|^{-1}\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of (
2.5
).

To prove (
2.6
), we apply Lemma
2.1
again

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle F,w\rangle.

This renders

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|F\|_{H^{-1}}.

Thus it follows

\displaystyle|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle F,w\rangle\lesssim\|F\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of (
2.6
).
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
and
w\in D_{k}
, it holds

(2.7)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=\|r^{-1}h\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(k^{2}-1)\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
h=r^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{8}}
. Moreover, we have the following estimate

(2.8)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\gtrsim\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}+k^{2}\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

We start with proving (
2.7
). Denote
h=r^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{8}}
. One can check

\displaystyle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w=-r^{2}h^{-1}\partial_{r}[r^{-2}h^{2}\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)]-\frac{2}{r}w^{\prime}+\frac{k^{2}+2}{r^{2}}w.

The identities below follow from integration by parts

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\Re\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}h\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle w^{\prime},\frac{2w}{r^{3}}\rangle+\langle\frac{k^{2}+2}{r^{2}}w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}h\partial_{r}(h^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(k^{2}-1)\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where in the last equality we use

(2.9)

\displaystyle-\Re\langle w^{\prime},\frac{2w}{r^{3}}\rangle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{3}}d|w|^{2}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{3|w|^{2}}{r^{4}}dr=-3\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Now we continue to prove (
2.8
). We first observe the following equality through integration by parts

\displaystyle\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})]w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle w^{\prime},\frac{2w}{r^{3}}\rangle+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

Together with (
2.9
) this gives

(2.10)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{13}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

Then we treat
|k|\geq 2
and
|k|=1
separately.

•

When
|k|\geq 2
, applying (
2.7
), we deduce

(2.11)

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\geq(k^{2}-1)\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim k^{2}\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining with (
2.11
), we hence obtain

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\gtrsim\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}+|k|)w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

•

When
|k|=1
, we write

\displaystyle-w^{\prime\prime}=-g^{-1}\partial_{r}(r^{2}g^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}g^{-1}w))-\frac{2}{r}w^{\prime}-g^{-1}[g^{\prime\prime}+(\frac{2}{r}g)^{\prime}]w

for any function
g
. Let
g
be a real function satisfying

(2.12)

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\prime\prime}}{g}+\frac{2}{r}\frac{g^{\prime}}{g}=-\frac{2}{r^{2}}.

The existence and explicit form of
g
are guaranteed by Lemma
A.1
in the Appendix. With (
2.9
) and (
2.12
), we deduce

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\|rg\partial_{r}(r^{-2}g^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-3\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle g^{-1}[g^{\prime\prime}+(\frac{2}{r}g)^{\prime}]w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|rg\partial_{r}(r^{-2}g^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle g^{-1}(g^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}g^{\prime})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle+\langle(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{5}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|rg\partial_{r}(r^{-2}g^{-1}w)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{7}{4r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

Together with (
2.10
), we prove

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\gtrsim\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

This completes the proof of Lemma
2.3
.
∎

In a similar fashion as proving Lemma
2.3
, we also obtain

Lemma 2.4
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
and
w\in D_{k}
, it holds

(2.13)

\displaystyle|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{H^{1}}+|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}},

(2.14)

\displaystyle\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{H^{1}}+|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{H^{-1}}.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
2.3
, one has

\displaystyle\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle\frac{F}{r},\frac{w}{r}\rangle,

which yields

\displaystyle|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we deduce

\displaystyle\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle\frac{F}{r},\frac{w}{r}\rangle\lesssim|k|^{-1}\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of (
2.13
).

Using Lemma
2.3
again, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle\frac{F}{r},\frac{w}{r}\rangle,

which gives

\displaystyle\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{H^{-1}}.

Therefore, we can conclude

\displaystyle|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Re\langle\frac{F}{r},\frac{w}{r}\rangle\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of (
2.14
).
∎

2.2.
Resolvent estimates in
L^{2}
space

We first derive the resolvent estimates from
L^{2}
to
L^{2}
.

Proposition 2.5
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in D_{k}
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
k,\beta_{k},\lambda
, such that the following estimate holds

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Let us first prove
|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F\|_{L^{2}}
. If
\lambda\leq 0
, with Lemma
2.1
we have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

Together with

\displaystyle|\Im\langle F,w\rangle|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle\frac{1}{r^{2}}w,w\rangle,

this gives

\displaystyle|\langle F,w\rangle|\gtrsim\langle(r^{2}+\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r^{2}})w,w\rangle\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Recall that
\beta_{k}=kB
and
|B|\geq 1
. Thus
|\beta_{k}|=|kB|\geq 1
for
|k|\geq 1
, and we have

(2.15)

\displaystyle|\langle F,w\rangle|\gtrsim|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

If
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\leq k^{2}
, utilizing Lemma
2.1
again, one obtains

(2.16)

\displaystyle|\langle F,w\rangle|\geq

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle\geq|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

It remains to deal with the scenario
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\geq k^{2}
. Denote
r_{0}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}
. The discussion can be divided into the below three cases:

(1)

Case of
|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{4}
.

With Lemma
2.1
we have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

Combining with

\displaystyle|\Im\langle F,w\rangle|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,w\rangle,

we deduce

\displaystyle|\langle F,w\rangle|\gtrsim\langle[(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})]w,w\rangle\geq\langle[r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})]w,w\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})\geq 2|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

we then obtain

(2.17)

\displaystyle|\langle F,w\rangle|\gtrsim|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(2)

Case of
r_{0}^{4}\leq|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{6}
.
The condition
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
implies that
r_{0}\geq 1
. Using Lemma
2.1
, we derive the estimate for
\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle\geq\|rw\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{4}\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}.

The estimate for
\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2})}^{2}
is more subtle. First, we choose
r_{-}\in(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{2r_{0}},\frac{r_{0}}{2})
such that the following inequality holds

(2.18)

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}(r_{-})|^{2}\leq 2r_{0}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Notice the imaginary part of
\langle F,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle
obeys

\displaystyle\Im\langle F,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}w-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w]+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-\partial_{r}^{2}w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle.

We hence prove

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle\leq\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|.

Using
\lambda=\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}
and
r_{-}\in(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{2r_{0}},\frac{r_{0}}{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{4}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,w\chi_{(0,r_{-})}\rangle\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,r_{-})}^{2}.

Therefore,
\|w\|_{L^{2}}
can be bounded as below

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,r_{-})}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(r_{-},\frac{r_{0}}{2})}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|)+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}.

Together with (
2.18
), Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}})+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}})+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+r_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{5}{4}})+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}.

This yields

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\min\{\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}},(\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}})^{\frac{4}{3}},r_{0}^{2}\}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

With
r_{0}^{4}\leq|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{6}
, i.e.
\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{4}{3}}}{r_{0}^{\frac{10}{3}}}\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\geq r_{0}^{2}
, we conclude

(2.19)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim r_{0}^{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(3)

Case of
|\beta_{k}|\geq r_{0}^{6}
.

We choose
r_{-}\in(r_{0}-\delta,r_{0}-\frac{\delta}{2})
and
r_{+}\in(r_{0}+\frac{\delta}{2},r_{0}+\delta)
such that the following inequality holds

(2.20)

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}(r_{-})|^{2}+|w^{\prime}(r_{+})|^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\delta}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Here
\delta\in(0,r_{0})
is a constant which will be determined later.

The following equality is direct

\displaystyle\Im\langle F,w(\chi_{(0,r_{-})}-\chi_{(r_{+},\infty)})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}w-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w]+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w(\chi_{(0,r_{-})}-\chi_{(r_{+},\infty)})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-\partial_{r}^{2}w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w(\chi_{(0,r_{-})}-\chi_{(r_{+},\infty)})\rangle.

This implies

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|(\int_{0}^{r_{-}}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr+\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|+|w^{\prime}(r_{+})w(r_{+})|.

With
r_{-}\in(r_{0}-\delta,r_{0}-\frac{\delta}{2})
and
r_{+}\in(r_{0}+\frac{\delta}{2},r_{0}+\delta)
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}=\frac{(r_{0}-r)(r_{0}+r)}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\geq\frac{r_{0}-r}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\in(0,r_{-}).

In a similar fashion, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}=\frac{(r-r_{0})(r+r_{0})}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\geq\frac{r-r_{0}}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\in(r_{+},2r_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}=\frac{(r-r_{0})(r+r_{0})}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\approx\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\geq 2r_{0}.

Combining these above inequalities, we get

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|+|w^{\prime}(r_{+})w(r_{+})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|(\int_{0}^{r_{-}}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr+\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}\big((0,r_{-})\cup(r_{+},\infty)\big)}^{2}.

Therefore,
\|w\|_{L^{2}}
obeys the estimate

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}((0,r_{-})\cup(r_{+},\infty))}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(r_{-},r_{+})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}\Big(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+|w^{\prime}(r_{-})w(r_{-})|+|w^{\prime}(r_{+})w(r_{+})|\Big)+\delta\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

Together with (
2.20
), Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
2.1
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}})+\delta\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}})+\delta\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}(\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{5}{4}}}{\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}})+\delta\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}.

It then follows

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\min\{\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}},(\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r_{0}^{3}})^{\frac{4}{3}},\delta^{-2}\}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Take
\delta=(\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|})^{\frac{1}{3}}
. The condition
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
yields

\displaystyle\delta=(\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|})^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq r_{0}.

With the basic equality
\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}=(\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r_{0}^{3}})^{\frac{4}{3}}=\delta^{-2}
, we obtain

(2.21)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim(\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{3}})^{\frac{2}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining (
2.15
), (
2.16
), (
2.17
), (
2.19
) and (
2.21
), we now establish the following resolvent estimate from
L^{2}
to
L^{2}

(2.22)

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
2.1
we have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle\gtrsim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which along with (
2.22
) implies the resolvent estimate from
H^{1}
to
L^{2}

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|\beta_{k}|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|F\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
2.5
.
∎

Now we move to establish the resolvent estimates from
L^{2}
to
H^{-1}
.

Proposition 2.6
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in D_{k}
, there exist constants
C,c_{2}>0
independent of
k,\beta_{k},\lambda
, such that the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}+|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}.

Proof.

From Lemma
2.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,w\rangle=\Re\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle,

with
c_{2}>0
to be determined later. This gives

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Thus we have

(2.23)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}.

To prove
|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}
, we proceed in the same fashion as for proving Proposition
2.5
. If
\lambda\leq 0
, using Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle,

Combining with

\displaystyle|\Im\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle\frac{1}{r^{2}}w,w\rangle,

and (
2.23
), we get

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim\langle(r^{2}+\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r^{2}})w,w\rangle\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle C(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Recall
|\beta_{k}|=|kB|\geq 1
. Choose
c_{2}>0
sufficiently small such that
Cc_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we can conclude

(2.24)

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

If
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\leq k^{2}
, by Lemma
2.1
and (
2.23
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Re\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle\geq|k|\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Recall
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
. Choose
c_{2}>0
being sufficiently small such that
Cc_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we can conclude

(2.25)

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

The remaining the scenario is when
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\geq k^{2}
. As the proof for Proposition
2.5
, we consider the following three cases. Recall
r_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}
.

(1)

Case of
|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{4}
.
Utilizing Lemma
2.1
again, it holds

\displaystyle\Re\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle.

Together with

\displaystyle|\Im\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,w\rangle,

this implies

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\langle[(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})]w,w\rangle\geq\langle[r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})]w,w\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})\geq 2|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

we then deduce

\displaystyle C(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\gtrsim|\langle F,w\rangle|\gtrsim\langle g(r)w,w\rangle\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Recall
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
. Choose
c_{2}>0
sufficiently small such that
Cc_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we can conclude

(2.26)

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(2)

Case of
r_{0}^{4}\leq|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{6}
.
Recall
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
, then we have
r_{0}\geq 1
. With Lemma
2.1
, we obtain the below estimate for
\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}

\displaystyle\Re\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rangle+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,w\rangle\geq\|rw\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{4}\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}.

As in Proposition
2.5
, the estimate for
\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2})}^{2}
is more subtle. First, we choose
r_{-}\in(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{r_{0}},\frac{r_{0}}{2})
so that the following inequality holds

(2.27)

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}(r_{-})|^{2}\leq r_{0}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Being slightly different from the proof of Proposition
2.5
, we require the multiplier for
H^{-1}
estimate to be of
C^{1}
regularity. We introduce a cutoff function
\rho(r)
as follows:

\displaystyle\rho(r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad r\in(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}}),\\
&\sin\big(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{1}{r_{-}-(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}})}(r_{-}-r)\big),\quad r\in(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}},r_{-}),\\
&0,\quad r\geq r_{-}.\end{aligned}\right.

Then we consider the following basic equality

\displaystyle\Im\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rho(r)\rangle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}w-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w]+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\rho(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-\partial_{r}^{2}w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\rho(r)\rangle.

This gives

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\rho(r)\rangle\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}+r_{0}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thus, with
\lambda=\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}
and
r_{-}\in(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{r_{0}},\frac{r_{0}}{2})
we obtain

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}+r_{0}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{4}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,w\rho(r)\rangle\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}})}^{2}.

Since

\displaystyle\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{1}}+r_{0}\|w\|_{L^{2}},

then
\|w\|_{L^{2}}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}})}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2}-\frac{2}{r_{0}},\frac{r_{0}}{2})}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(\frac{r_{0}}{2},\infty)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}(\|w\|_{H^{1}}+r_{0}\|w\|_{L^{2}})+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Together with (
2.27
), Lemma
A.2
and (
2.23
) this gives

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}+r_{0}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+r_{0}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r_{0}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+r_{0}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r_{0}}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\Big).

Thus we obtain

\displaystyle C(c_{2}\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}}{r_{0}}+c_{2}\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}}{r_{0}^{2}})\leq C(c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{-\frac{1}{6}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}+c_{2}),

Recall
|\beta_{k}|\geq 1
. Choose
c_{2}>0
satisfying

\displaystyle C(c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{-\frac{1}{6}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}+c_{2})\leq\frac{1}{2},

we can conclude

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim\min\{\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}},(\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{3}})^{2},r_{0}^{2}\}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Noting that
r_{0}^{4}\leq|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{6}
, i.e.
\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{4}{3}}}{r_{0}^{\frac{10}{3}}}\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\geq r_{0}^{2}
, we hence arrive at

(2.28)

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim r_{0}^{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(3)

Case of
|\beta_{k}|\geq r_{0}^{6}
.
Similarly, as in the previous case, we choose
r_{-}\in(r_{0}-\delta,r_{0}-\frac{\delta}{2})
and
r_{+}\in(r_{0}+\frac{\delta}{2},r_{0}+\delta)
such that the following inequality holds

(2.29)

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}(r_{-})|^{2}+|w^{\prime}(r_{+})|^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\delta}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

Here
\delta>0
is a constant which will be determined later. As in Proposition
2.5
, we define a
C^{1}
cutoff function
\rho
with domain
(0,\infty)
as follows

\displaystyle\rho(r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad r\in(0,r_{0}-2\delta),\\
&\sin\big(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{1}{r_{-}-(r_{0}-2\delta)}(r_{-}-r)\big),\quad r\in(r_{0}-2\delta,r_{-}),\\
&0,\quad r\in(r_{-},r_{+}),\\
&\sin\big(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{1}{r_{0}+2\delta-r_{+}}(r_{+}-r)\big),\quad r\in(r_{+},r_{0}+2\delta),\\
&-1,\quad r\geq r_{0}+2\delta.\end{aligned}\right.

Observing

\displaystyle\Im\langle F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w,w\rho(r)\rangle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-[\partial_{r}^{2}w-(\frac{k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{16}-\frac{1}{2})w]+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\rho(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\langle-\partial_{r}^{2}w+i\beta_{k}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\lambda)w,w\rho(r)\rangle,

we obtain

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|(\int_{0}^{r_{0}-2\delta}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr+\int_{r_{0}+2\delta}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}+\delta^{-1}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}.

With
r_{-}\in(r_{0}-\delta,r_{0}-\frac{\delta}{2})
and
r_{+}\in(r_{0}+\frac{\delta}{2},r_{0}+\delta)
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}=\frac{(r_{0}-r)(r_{0}+r)}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\geq\frac{r_{0}-r}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\in(0,r_{-}).

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}=\frac{(r-r_{0})(r+r_{0})}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\geq\frac{r-r_{0}}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r^{2}r_{0}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}},\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\in(r_{+},2r_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}=\frac{(r-r_{0})(r+r_{0})}{r^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\approx\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{r_{0}^{3}},\quad\textrm{for any}\ r\geq 2r_{0}.

Plugging in all these above inequalities, we deduce

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}+\delta^{-1}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|(\int_{0}^{r_{0}-2\delta}(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr+\int_{r_{0}+2\delta}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}})|w(r)|^{2}dr)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}\big((0,r_{0}-2\delta)\cup(r_{0}+2\delta,\infty)\big)}^{2}.

Since

\displaystyle\|\rho w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{1}}+\delta^{-1}\|w\|_{L^{2}},

then
\|w\|_{L^{2}}
can be bounded as follows

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}\big((0,r_{0}-2\delta)\cup(r_{0}+2\delta,\infty)\big)}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}(r_{0}-2\delta,r_{0}+2\delta)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}+\delta^{-1}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})+\delta\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

Employing (
2.29
), Lemma
A.2
and (
2.23
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}\Big(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}\Big)+\delta\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}\Big(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}\Big)+\delta\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|\delta}\Big(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}+c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\delta(\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}\Big].

Take
\delta=(\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|})^{\frac{1}{3}}
, then the condition
|\beta_{k}|\geq\max\{r_{0}^{6},1\}
yields

\displaystyle\delta=(\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|})^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq r_{0}.

Notice that
|\beta_{k}|\geq r_{0}^{6}
, we deduce

\displaystyle C(c_{2}\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\delta}+\sqrt{c_{2}}\frac{r_{0}^{3}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{5}{6}}\delta^{2}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}\delta|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}})=C(c_{2}\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}}+2\sqrt{c_{2}}\frac{r_{0}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}})\leq C(c_{2}+2\sqrt{c_{2}}),

Choose
c_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle C(c_{2}+2\sqrt{c_{2}})\leq\frac{1}{2},

we have

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim\min\{\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}},(\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta^{2}}{r_{0}^{3}})^{2},\delta^{-2}\}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

With the basic equality
\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta}{r_{0}^{3}}=(\frac{|\beta_{k}|\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r_{0}^{3}})^{\frac{4}{3}}=\delta^{-2}
, we conclude

(2.30)

\displaystyle\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\gtrsim(\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{3}})^{\frac{2}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining (
2.24
), (
2.25
), (
2.26
), (
2.28
) and (
2.30
), we therefore establish the following resolvent estimate from
L^{2}
to
H^{-1}

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}.

Applying (
2.23
), we have

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}+\sqrt{c_{2}}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
2.6
.
∎

The following proposition can be derived directly from Proposition
2.5
and Proposition
2.6
if we choose
c_{2}>0
being small enough.

Proposition 2.7
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in D_{k}
, there exist constants
C,c_{2}>0
independent of
k,\beta_{k},\lambda
, such that the following estimates hold

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\|w\|_{H^{1}}+|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|F-c_{2}|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}w\|_{H^{-1}}.

2.3.
Resolvent estimates in the weighted
L^{2}
space
X

To prove sharp decaying estimates for the nonlinear problem, we design and employ a new energy space. Here we introduce a weighted space
L^{2}
space as follows

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X}=(\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|w|^{2}}{r^{2}}dr)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As we proceed in the last subsection, we start with deriving the resolvent estimates from
L^{2}(\frac{1}{r^{2}})
to
L^{2}(\frac{1}{r^{2}})
.

Proposition 2.8
.

For any
|k|\geq 1
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in D_{k}
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
k,\beta_{k},\lambda
, such that the following inequality holds

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}+|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

We first prove
|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}
. The discussion is analogous to proofs of Proposition
2.5
and Proposition
2.6
. If
\lambda\leq 0
, using Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\Re\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\gtrsim\langle(\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}+r^{2})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

This together with

\displaystyle|\Im\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle|\geq|\beta_{k}|\langle\frac{1}{r^{2}}w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle,

yields

(2.31)

\displaystyle\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\langle(r^{2}+\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r^{2}})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

If
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\leq k^{2}
. With Lemma
2.3
, we have

(2.32)

\displaystyle\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim|k|\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}.

If
\lambda>0
and
|\beta_{k}|\geq k^{2}
, we consider the following three cases. Recall
r_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}
.

(1)

Case of
|\beta_{k}|\leq r_{0}^{4}
.
Via integration by parts we get

\displaystyle\Im\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle=-2\Im\langle\frac{w^{\prime}}{r},\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle+\beta_{k}\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

Together with Lemma
2.3
this gives

\displaystyle|\beta_{k}|\langle(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle\leq|\Im\langle F,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle|+2\|\frac{w^{\prime}}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{w}{r^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}.

Employing Lemma
2.3
again, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{F}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{w}{r}\|_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\langle[r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})]w,\frac{w}{r^{2}}\rangle.

Since

\displaystyle r^{2}+|\beta_{k}|(\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}})\geq 2|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{|\beta_{k}|}{r_{0}^{2}}\geq|\beta_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

we conclude

(2.33)
